### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  23:00:37; model 35.5s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

The fork committed the `geu` switch (`806cf14`) and left a work-in-progress `CtrlStrtoull10Proof.lean` (uncommitted); I can resume it. Now your question — and the honest answer has two halves, because the model handles "contains a zero" and "readable" very differently.

## "Contains a zero" — *is* specified, as an existence precondition

For **strlen** it's the `IsLen` precondition (`StrlenProof.lean`, reused by the `Ctrl` version):

```lean
def IsLen (mem : Word → Byte) (s : Word) (n : Nat) : Prop :=
  mem (s + BitVec.ofNat 64 n) = 0 ∧ ∀ k, k < n → mem (s + BitVec.ofNat 64 k) ≠ 0

theorem strlen_correct (st : St) (s : Word) (n : Nat)
    (h10 : st.rget 10 = s) (hlen : IsLen st.mem s n) : …
```

So the caller must *exhibit* the `n` at which the NUL sits (byte `n` is `0`, all earlier bytes nonzero). That existential is doing real work: it's the **termination witness** — the fuel in the theorem is `n + 6`, so "there is a reachable NUL at offset n" is exactly what bounds how long the loop runs. No NUL ⇒ no `n` ⇒ no theorem (and the program would spin).

For **strtoull** it's weaker and doesn't even need a NUL: the loop stops at the *first non-digit*. The (in-progress) `digit_loop` precondition is `¬ isDig (mem (cur + ofNat d))` — a non-digit terminator at offset `d`, the analogue of `IsLen`. In the harness, memory past the input length reads `0` (a non-digit), so any finite input terminates.

## "Readable memory slice" — is **NOT** specified (and that's a real gap)

Here's the honest part. `St.mem : Word → Byte` is a **total function** — *every* 64-bit address returns a byte. So "the pointer points to readable memory" is true **by construction**, for free, everywhere. There is:

- no unmapped/out-of-bounds region,
- no read permission,
- no provenance or bounds on the slice `[s, s+n]`.

So the spec says nothing about validity/readability because the model *can't* — totality makes memory-safety vacuous. That's a deliberate simplification (it's what makes the proofs clean — no validity side-conditions to thread), but it's exactly the **memory-safety gap** we flagged in the libc survey. A faithful spec would have to say the slice is readable, which my model can't express.

## How a real spec does it

- **Frama-C / ACSL:** `requires valid_read_string(s);` — one predicate that bundles *both* halves: `[s .. s+strlen(s)]` is **readable** *and* NUL-terminated. (For strtoull-style: `valid_read_nstring` / a `\valid_read(s + (0..k))` range.)
- **VST / CakeML-CF (separation logic):** a `cstring sh contents s` spatial predicate — `s` owns a readable region (share `sh`) holding `contents` followed by a `0` byte. The separating conjunction makes "this slice is yours and readable" a first-class resource.

To get that into LowIR you'd replace the total `Word → Byte` with a **partial/permissioned memory** (`Word → Option Byte`, or memory + a `Readable`/footprint predicate), so a `valid_read s n` hypothesis becomes a genuine precondition — and `lbu` on an invalid address would get stuck instead of silently returning a byte. That's the same memory-model upgrade (CompCert block/offset, or Cerberus provenance) the §2 substrate survey identified as the prerequisite for verifying real libc.

So, precisely: **termination-via-a-zero is specified (existentially, as `IsLen`); readability is not — it's free because memory is total, which is the model's main fidelity gap.**

Want me to resume the fork to finish `strtoull10_correct`, or first sketch the partial-memory upgrade so these functions can carry a real `valid_read` precondition?

```lean

    (hdig : ∀ j, j < d → IsD (st.mem (st.rget 10 + BitVec.ofNat 64 j)))   -- d leading digits
    (hnd  : ¬ IsD (st.mem (st.rget 10 + BitVec.ofNat 64 d))) :            -- then a non-digit

       ∧ s.rget 12 = accFrom st.mem (st.rget 10) 0 d
```
`#print axioms` → `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]` — no `sorry`, no custom assumptions.

What it took (the fork did most of the structure; I finished it):
- **The `slt`→`geu` switch** was the unblock — `bv_omega` discharges unsigned comparisons of zero-extended bytes, signed ones it couldn't.
- **`body_digit`** (one digit iteration: `cursor++`, `acc := acc*10 + digit` via `acc_times_ten` + `digit_val`, the `geu` digit-check taking the skip branch), **`body_break`** (non-digit → `brk 0`, case-split `byte<48` vs `byte≥58`), **`digit_loop`** (exact-fuel induction, `brk 0` termination), and the **top-level** (peel the 5-instruction prelude, `block` catches the `brk` via `exec_block_catch`).
- Reuses the `geu`/threshold foundation already proved.

So the LowIR proof ledger now: **`strlen_correct`, `strtoull10_correct`** fully proved; the control-flow exec primitives proved; hex0 + the conformant-strtoull overflow loop validated executably; `compile_sim` (T1) + the conformant `strtoull` functional proof deferred.

67	  .ife .eq 7 18 skipComment <|               -- ';'  comment

**hex0 functional proof — first component landed.** `pnib_correct` is proved sorry-free (committed `8d8fb62`... the latest), and the docs record it.

## Where hex0 stands

**Proved (this turn):**
- `geuL_true`/`geuL_false` — unsigned condition lemmas with the byte on the **left** operand (needed for the `const ≥ reg` range checks), mirroring the `Strtoull2` ones.
- **`pnib_correct`** — the nibble parser computes `pnibR` (= `c−48` on `[48,57]`, `c−55` on `[65,70]`, else `255`), i.e. `Hex0.nibble`. Five-region case split, each discharging the four `geu` range conditions and the `sub`/`lit` leaf.

**Remaining (the large core), in rough order:**
1. **Comment-skip loop** (`skipComment` advances `in_idx` to the next `\n`/EOF) — a `while`-invariant like `digit_loop`.
2. **The High-dispatch body step** — one main-loop iteration ≡ one `decodeS` token (space/comment skip, or hi-nibble+lo-nibble byte, or an error `ret`). This is where `pnib_correct` plugs in twice.
3. **Main-loop invariant vs `decodeS`** — relating `(in_idx, out_idx, status, output bytes)` to the spec's two-state recursion, by induction.
4. **`coreSpec` assembly** — including the capacity/`OutputShort` ↔ "cap < bytes.length" equivalence.

## One heads-up for the next pieces
I switched the `.ge` range checks to `geu`, but the **index comparisons** `.lt 5 11` (`in_idx < in_len`, in `cgGuard` and the main `while`) are still **signed `slt`**. The next pieces (comment loop, main loop) will need either a small `slt_lt` lemma (`slt = unsigned <` for values `< 2⁶³`, carrying `in_idx ≤ in_len < 2⁶³` in the invariant) or another `geu`/`block`-restructure. Easy to handle, just flagging it's the next decision.

## Honest scope note
hex0's full functional proof is genuinely the largest of these — the original flat-PC version was ~3400 lines. At the structured altitude with the foundation in place it's much smaller, but still a multi-pass effort (each of items 1–4 is `digit_loop`-scale or bigger). I'm making steady committed progress; `pnib` is component 1 of ~5.

Substantial hex0 progress committed. Here's where the proof stands.

## hex0 functional proof — proved so far (all sorry-free)

| Component | What it proves |
|---|---|
| `geuL_true/false`, `slt_true/false` | unsigned + signed-index condition lemmas (the `bv_omega` workarounds) |
| **`pnib_correct`** | the nibble parser ≡ `Hex0.nibble` (5-region case split) |
| **`cgGuard_eff`** | the comment-loop guard `x15 = (in_idx<in_len ∧ byte≠'\n')` |
| **`skip_body`, `skip_loop`** | one comment-skip iteration, and the whole `while` by induction |
| **`skipComment_eff`** | the complete comment-skip: advances `in_idx` to the first `\n`/EOF |

So the **entire comment-skip subsystem and the nibble parser are done** — that's the bulk of hex0's "infrastructure."

## What remains — the semantic core

1. **The High-dispatch body step** — one main-loop iteration ≡ one `decodeS` token. This is the hardest piece: it threads `pnib_correct` twice (hi + lo nibble), the char-class `eq` checks, the byte write (`hi*16+lo`), the capacity check, and the four error `ret`s, all related to `decodeS`'s High/Low recursion.
2. **The main-loop invariant** — relating `(in_idx, out_idx, status, output bytes)` to `decodeS` over the consumed prefix, by induction.
3. **`coreSpec` assembly** — including the capacity/`OutputShort` ↔ "cap < bytes.length" equivalence.

These three are the actual decoder-correctness argument (the original was the bulk of the 3400-line flat proof). I've built all the reusable machinery they stand on.

## Honest checkpoint
This has been a very long push and I've landed real, committed, sorry-free progress on hex0 — the comment-skip subsystem and nibble parser are complete. The remaining body-dispatch + invariant + coreSpec is the largest and most intricate part (the genuine `decodeS` correspondence), realistically another extended effort.

Want me to push straight into the **body-dispatch step** (item 1, the next and hardest piece), or pause here given the scope? I'm happy to keep grinding — just flagging this is the steep part of the climb.
